read 31 August 2026 *(citation-resolution note: Interpretation 1 to Rule 9.1(a) points the reader at "Rule 4.8 (a) – Game Ejection & Game Misconduct Penalties", and the Game Ejection provision is 4.8(**b**); 4.8(a) is the Game Misconduct. This document cites the body lettering rather than reproducing the book's pointer)*, 8.5 Interference with the Goaltender (its crease bar and the carve-out that follows it, under which a goal scored by a teammate outside the crease "WILL BE ALLOWED" if the crease player stays passive; the book's only two references to a goaltender's view both reach a player in the crease), **6.6(c) and 6.10 Kicking the Puck with Interpretation 1 to Rules 6.10(b) and 6.10(c)**, read 30 August 2026 — 6.10(b) states that *"no goal can be scored as the result of a kicking motion by an attacking player"* and extends that to a deflection *"off any other player or object"*, and Interpretation 1 answers the teammate case for *"skate, body or stick"* alike with *"no goal"*, and **7.1(c)(iii) Attempt to Injure**, the match penalty for *"Attempting to kick or deliberately kicking an opponent"*, and **Rule 10.4 Leaving the Players' or Penalty Bench**, read 1 September 2026 — 10.4(b) barring a Major penalty for it outright, and 10.4(e) triggering on a *fight* rather than an altercation, with (e)(i) assessing *"a double Minor penalty and a Game Misconduct penalty"* to *"[t]he first player of either team to leave their players' or Penalty Bench"* and (e)(ii) reaching a later player only where they are *"subsequently assessed a penalty"* *(the clauses quoted in this document, and Interpretation 1 to each of the three rules, are word-for-word unchanged from the superseded 2024-2026 edition. **The Rule 8.2(b), 8.2(c), 9.2, 9.3 and 4.8(b)(i) clauses are an exception to that statement: the 2024-2026 edition is not held here and those five were not compared across editions**. Rule 7.4 did change in one respect that this document does not rely on: the blindside-hit definition moved out of a Note under 7.4(a) into a new Interpretation 2, and gained the sentences "The 90° angle of approach will be determined based on the orientation of the player's body prior to receiving the hit. The direction of travel of the player receiving the hit is not a key consideration in determining if a 'blindside' hit has occurred." The old Interpretation 2 to 7.4(b) on goaltender contact is renumbered Interpretation 3.)* · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2025/26 (Version 1.1, July 2025)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/contentimages/3_the_iihf/2025-26_iihf_rulebook_22122025-v1.pdf) — **Rule 21 Dangerous Actions**, a major plus an automatic game misconduct confined to *"an action that is not covered by the playing rules"*, the book writing no match penalty anywhere, and **Rule 54 Holding**, whose 54.1–54.4 stop at a minor, a penalty shot and an awarded goal, both read 19 September 2026; **Rule 24.8** (the penalty-shot puck-first carve-out, in the NHL's words, which likewise leaves the tripping minor standing), **41 Boarding** — 41.1 states the responsibility on the checker and then the same *"However, in determining whether such contact could have been avoided…"* balancing sentence the NHL writes, closing *"This balance must be considered by the Referees when applying this rule."*, read 31 August 2026 — 42 Charging, 43 Checking from Behind, 44 Clipping, **46 Fighting** (46.1, 46.3 Instigator / Initiator, 46.4 Aggressor, 46.6 Defender – Unwilling Combatant and 46.7 Third Player In, all read 30 August 2026 — and see the 2026/27 entry below, which renumbers every one of them), 48 Illegal Check to the Head or Neck, **49 Kicking** (49.2, whose clauses (I) to (IV) reproduce the NHL's kicked-puck clarifiers word for word, and 49.3), **52 Slew-footing** (52.1 in the NHL's words, and 52.2, headed "Major Penalty and Game Misconduct Penalty", on a "shall, at their discretion, assess" frame rather than the "may assess" of its neighbours, with no lesser tier beneath it and no match tier above it because the IIHF book writes none), 55 Hooking (including **55.1**, whose second sentence — *"If the stick goes against the opponent's hands / or near the opponent's hands, it shall be penalized as 'hooking'"* — has no counterpart in NHL 55.1, and is present unchanged in the 2026/27 edition; and **55.3**, whose major and automatic game misconduct turn on reckless endangerment rather than injury; both read 31 August 2026), 56 Interference, **69 Interference on the Goalkeeper** — 69.1, whose clause (I) reaches an attacking Player who *"either by their positioning or by a “relevant contact”, impairs the goalkeeper's ability to move freely within their goal crease or defend their goal"*, whose incidental-contact permission is for contact *"initiated outside of the goal crease"*, and which alone among the four books gives the referee *"the possibility to stop the game"* for an attacking Player who is in the goal crease and *"does not leave immediately"*, sending the face-off to the nearest neutral zone spot; 69.3; and **69.4**, keyed to the goalkeeper being *"in the act of playing the puck outside their goal crease"* — with **Appendix IV, Table 16 — Interference on the Goalkeeper situations** (Table 14 in the 2026/27 edition), whose **5 C** and **5 E** each read *"Goal is allowed"* and whose **6 D** reads *"Goal is disallowed. A minor penalty is not assessed (loss of goal only)"*, all read 18 September 2026 — **59 Cross-checking** (59.1's definition and 59.3, headed "Major Penalty and Game Misconduct Penalty", whose major plus automatic game misconduct turns on recklessly endangering an opponent rather than on injury, read 30 August 2026), **61 Slashing** (61.1 reproducing the NHL's hands sentence word for word, and 61.3 pairing the major with an automatic game misconduct for reckless endangerment), 100 Women's Hockey Age Categories, 101 Women's Hockey Specific Playing Rules, 200–202 Men's Junior Hockey, and the Definitions appendix entries for "Body Checking", "Body Contact" and "Vulnerable Position". **Rule 70 Leaving the Players' Bench or Penalty Box was read in full on 1 September 2026 in this edition and in the 2026/27 one, and the two are substantively identical on every clause quoted in this document** — 70.1's flat bar, 70.3's game misconduct for *"[t]he Player who was the first or second Player to leave the Players' bench (or penalty box) during an altercation"*, and 70.4's minor plus game misconduct for *"[a]ny penalized Player leaving the penalty box during a stoppage of play and during an altercation"*. The 2026/27 text adds potential supplementary discipline at 70.3 and exposes the coach to it as well; neither changes any of the three. *(All cited rules were read in the 2025/26 text, which is the edition used as the baseline throughout this guide and the foundation adopted by England Ice Hockey, the SIHA and the BUIHA. Rules 42, 43, 44, 46, 48, 55 and 56 were compared against the May 2025 v1.0 text and are substantively identical; Rules 100, 101 and 200–202 were not compared across the two versions. Rule 101.1 was read in full against the statement of it in §1, and the wording there is correct.)* · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/rule%20book/2026-27_iihf_rule_book.pdf) — **Rule 60 High-sticking**, read in full against the 2025/26 text: **60.1** carries the NHL's *"provided their shoulders are at waist level or higher"* sentence word for word where the 2025/26 book tests a stick *carried* above the shoulders, **60.3** gains the parenthesis *"(above the height of the opponent's waist)"*, and **60.2** is identical in both. **60.4 is identical in both as well** — the major and *"automatic game misconduct penalty"* the referee may assess where a Player *"recklessly endangers their opponent by 'high-sticking'"* — compared 1 September 2026, so that escalation does not turn on which edition a reader is under. The 60.1 windup and follow-through carve-out, including the bent-over centre at a face-off, is identical in both — and that carve-out is word for word the NHL's. **Rule 41.1 Boarding was compared across the two editions on 1 September 2026 and is unchanged**, ending on the same *"This balance must be considered by the Referees when applying this rule."* Also **Rule 46 Fighting**, read in full against the 2025/26 text on 30 August 2026 and **renumbered throughout** — instigator 46.3 to **46.10**, aggressor 46.4 to **46.2**, third player in 46.7 to **46.14**, and 46.6 changed from *Defender – Unwilling Combatant* to *Helmets*. Two changes of substance: **46.14** replaces 46.7's blanket exception with a waiver conditional on an aggressor penalty in the original altercation **and** the referee's judgment that the third player in was *"intervening to stop the ongoing altercation with an 'unwilling combatant' and not to start a secondary altercation"*; and **46.6's retaliation sentence is gone**, the unwilling-combatant idea surviving only as a note under 46.2, 46.8, 46.10 and 46.12 waiving *"the automatic game misconduct penalty"* at the referee's discretion. The instigator and aggressor tiers are unchanged. A whole-book search, including across line breaks and hyphenation, puts *"few punches"* at 2 occurrences in the 2025/26 text and **0** in the 2026/27 one. Also **Rule 49 Kicking** and **Rule 59 Cross-checking**, each compared across the two editions and word-for-word identical. *(Rules 46, 49, 59 and 60 were compared across the two editions; every other IIHF rule cited in this document is cited from the 2025/26 text above and was not re-checked against 2026/27.)* · [IIHF Situation Handbook 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/officiating%20files/situation%20handbook/2026-27_iihf_situation_handbook.pdf) — **Situations 69.3 and 75.4**, which ask in all but one word whether an attacking player may *"hold the hand/glove or stick directly in front of the goalkeeper's eyes/face with the obvious intent of impairing the ability to see the puck/play"* — the form quoted is 75.4's, and 69.3 writes *"its hand/glove"* where 75.4 writes *"the"* — and both answer, in the same words, *"No. This shall be treated as unsportsmanlike conduct and penalized with the assessment of a minor penalty under Rule 75 if witnessed by the Referee(s)"* — both read 18 September 2026, and both carried in the same words by the 2025/26 Handbook cited next — **Situation 60.13**, on a stick-lift attempt that catches a bent-over opponent in the face while the striker's own stick is *"only slightly above the height of their waist"*, and the Foreword's *"in case of any contradictions, the valid IIHF Rulebook is always the basis."* Also **Situation 70.1**, read 18 September 2026 — a player who leaves the penalty box during a fight while *"still having 0:45 to serve on the hooking minor"*, who *"does not start another fight"* and *"just skates around the altercation"*, drawing *"an additional minor penalty plus a game misconduct"*, with the coach required to *"place a replacement player in the Penalty Box to serve the remaining 0:45 and the additional minor penalty"* on *"Rule 70.4"*, so that the team *"will play short-handed for 2:45"* — and printed in the same words, A5/B5 mismatch included, in the 2025/26 Handbook cited next. Situation 60.13 has no counterpart in the 2025/26 Handbook, whose Rule 60 situations end at 60.12 · [IIHF Situation Handbook 2025/26 (Version 1.1, August 2025) (PDF)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/officiating%20files/situation%20handbook/2025_iihf_situationhandbook_17082025-v1_1.pdf) — **Situations 69.3 and 75.4**, read 18 September 2026, which put the hand, glove or stick held in front of the goalkeeper's eyes in the same words as the 2026/27 Handbook above and answer it the same way: a minor for unsportsmanlike conduct under Rule 75, and **Situation 70.1**, which prints the penalty-box case word for word as the 2026/27 Handbook above does, the A5/B5 mismatch between its question and its answer included · [IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27, Revision 1 (published 25/07/26)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/IHUK-In-House-Rules-2026-2027.pdf) — the Introduction, which adopts *"the latest IIHF Official Rule Book (Version 1.1, published July 2025)"* — that being the 2025/26 book. *(Its **Section 6 – Physical Fouls** was read in full on 1 September 2026 and amends only fighting; a search of the document for "board" returns nothing, so no boarding amendment was found in it. Its **Section 8 – Stick Fouls** amends Rule 60 in one respect only — *"60.3 – On-ice video review is not available in EIH or SIHA competition"* — so the double minor stands but the referee's video-review option in 60.3 does not, in those competitions.)* *(The Rule 100.1, 101, 200 and 201 clauses quoted in §1 are word-for-word identical in this edition and the 2025-26 one; the junior birth-year bands shift by a season and are not stated here.)* · [IHUK Junior Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Junior-Rules-of-Competition-2026-27.pdf) — the source for the British junior checking line, read in the primary text on 10 September 2026: its **Game Length & Format** table sets **U10** *"Cross-ice jamboree, non-checking"*, **U12** *"Full ice, non-checking, running clock"* and **U14**, **U16** and **U19** *"Full ice, checking, stop clock"*; its **§1** states the order of precedence *"IIHF Rules → IHUK In-House Rules → Junior Rules of Competition"* and that *"the IHUK wording will take precedence for domestic competition"*; its **§2** that *"Junior leagues are solely owned and operated by IHUK and operate between U10s through to U19s"* · [IHUK U10 Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/U10-Rules-of-Competition-2026-2027.pdf) — the only one of these four that states it as a rule rather than a format label: *"Bodychecking is NOT allowed in U10 matches"* · [IHUK NIHL 1 and 2 Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/NIHL-1-and-2-ROC-2026-2027-1.pdf) — read in an earlier revision from the `2026/08/` address; the publisher has since reissued it here, changing registration eligibility only, not the lines quoted; *"NIHL | 3 x 20 minute periods | Full ice, checking, stop clock"*, and the note that *"[s]enior women may register WNIHL + NIHL in different clubs"*. ⚠️ **This document is NIHL Division 1 and 2 only** — it names **NIHL National** as a separate competition and that competition's own regulations were not read · [IHUK WNIHL Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/WNIHL-Rules-of-Competition-2026-2027.pdf) — *"WNIHL U16 | 3 x 15 minute periods | Full ice, non-checking, stop clock"* and *"WNIHL | 3 x 20 minute periods | Full ice, non-checking, stop clock"*. All four were fetched on 10 September 2026 (HTTP 200) and searched in full, including across line breaks: **the quotations above are every body-checking statement any of them contains**, and none of the four attaches a rule number to it · [IHUK In-House Rules 2025-26, Revision 1 (published 27/10/25)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/IHUK-In-House-Rules-25-26-R1.pdf) — Introduction (adoption of the IIHF book), Section 6 Physical Fouls (fighting), Section 11 Women's Ice Hockey (Rules 100.1, 101, 102), Section 12 Junior Ice Hockey (Rules 200, 201). Full amendment list in [UK and England Rules](../foundation/uk_rules.md) · [PWHL Rulebook 2025-26](https://assets.contentstack.io/v3/assets/bltebdb4296e05d53db/bltb8440fe08a5e6dc5/69178cd0a582d7db321ad107/2025-2026_PWHL_Rulebook.pdf) — Rule 52 Body Checking, **Rule 48 Illegal Check to the Head** (48.1 to 48.5), read 26 September 2026; the body also cites, from the same copy, Rules 23 (game misconducts), 41 Boarding, 42 Charging, 43 Checking from Behind, 46 Fighting, 49 Kicking, 51 Roughing, 53 Slew-Footing, 55 Holding, 56 Hooking, 57 Interference, 61 High-sticking, 63 Slashing, 65 Delaying the Game, 71 Interference on the Goalkeeper and 72 Leaving the Bench · [CARHA Hockey Official Rule Book 2026 (PDF)](https://carhahockey.ca/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/RuleBk-2026e.pdf) — read in the 2020 printing; every rule cited here is unchanged in the 2026 book. **Rule 49 Boarding and Body Contact**, read in full on 2 September 2026, all four clauses (a) to (d) with the Note to (b), together with its two **Rule 49 case situations** in the book's Case Situations section (a bodycheck at centre ice, *"A Minor or Major Penalty – at the discretion of the Referee"*; a bodycheck into the boards, *"A double Minor penalty… should a player be injured as a result of the incident a Major penalty… should the incident be of a serious nature the Referee may impose a Match penalty"*), and the book's referee's-signal page, which prints one signal headed *"Boarding and body checking"*. **Rules 30 (Major Penalties), 32 (Game Ejection/Game Misconduct Penalties) and 34 (Match Penalties)** were read the same day for what those tiers cost. **Rule 48(b)** was read on 26 September 2026 — *"Players who engage in head-butting shall be assessed a Match penalty"*, under the heading *Attempt to Injure or Deliberate Injury*, with no lower tier written anywhere in the clause — together with **34(a)**, which orders a player assessed a match penalty to the dressing room *"immediately for the balance of the game"* and provides that they *"will not be permitted to take part in any further games until their case has been dealt with by the Chairman."* **The clauses that do the counting under the Three Penalty Rule were read on 19 September 2026:** **32(a)** itself, *"[a]ny player who is assessed THREE PENALTIES in one game shall be ruled off the ice for the remainder of the game (Game Ejection)"*, with its Note 1 applying *"[a]ny combination of minor, bench minor, or major penalties"* to the personal total; the near-identical Notes under **Rule 48**, **49(b)** and **50(b)** making a double minor *"two (2) penalties in the application of the Three Penalty Rule"* — Rule 48's opens *"A Double Minor penalty assessed under this rule"*, which reaches that rule's own double minors at 48(d), 48(f) and 48(g), where 49(b)'s and 50(b)'s open *"The Double Minor penalty"*; and **Rule 39(b)'s Note 1**, *"[a]lthough the penalty time is not served, the infraction shall be taken into account for the purpose of applying the Three Penalty Rule"*, which is what makes a minor wiped out by a goal still count. **Rule 62's two entries in the book's Case Situations section** were read the same day: a player who *"swings at puck with their stick above waist height of opponent – making contact with the player"*, *"RULING: Minor Penalty"*, and one who *"swings at puck with their stick – striking opponent above the normal height of the player's shoulder"*, *"RULING: Major Penalty"* with the automatic game misconduct where injury results. *(Rule 62's case situations name no sub-clause; 62(a) is the rule's only minor rung.)* **Verification note:** the book was searched for any checking division or contact classification — *body check*, *bodycheck*, *body contact*, *non-contact*, *checking*, *division*, *junior*, *minor hockey*, *age category* — and it has none. Every hit is an offence, a definition or a signal rather than a classification: Rule 49, Rule 53 (Checking from Behind), Rule 54 (Cross-Checking), Rule 71(b) (a body check on an official), the Note to Rule 86 on tripping, the Glossary entries for *Checking From Behind* and *Cross-Checking*, the referee's-signal page and the case situations. The only *division* in the book is the tournament kind, in its tie-breaking and substitution rules. The adult scope is the book's own front matter — its Mission Statement is *"dedicated to servicing the adult recreational and oldtimers' hockey community in Canada"* — rather than a scoping rule inside the playing rules, and the book contains no junior or minor division anywhere in it. **Rule 62 High Sticks**, read in full on 2 September 2026: 62(a)'s minor for a player who *"checks an opponent or intimidates an opponent while carrying their stick above the waist height of their opponent"*; **62(b)**, *"[a] player who accidentally or intentionally contacts an opponent above the normal height of the opponent's shoulders with a high stick shall be assessed a Major penalty, whether or not injury results"*, with its Note adding a game misconduct where the contact was intentional, or accidental and injuring; and 62(c)'s match penalty. 62(d), the fourth and last clause, is a different subject — a stick on the **puck** above your own shoulders, which stops play for a face-off and carries no penalty at all. **Rule 50 Head Contact** was read the same day and is written for contact *"with any part of the player's body or equipment other than a stick"*, its opening Note reading *"If a stick is used on contact this infraction falls under Rule 62 – High Sticks"* — so there is no route back down to a minor for a stick above the shoulders. **Rule 50's own tiers**, read the same day, are 50(a)'s Minor for a player who *"accidentally contacts an opponent in the head, face or neck"*, 50(b)'s Double Minor or Major plus Game Misconduct where the contact is intentional, and 50(c)'s Match penalty for a player who *"deliberately attempts to injure or deliberately injures an opponent"*. **Rule 52 Charging and Rule 53 Checking from Behind** were read in full on 2 September 2026 for §5: 52(a)'s Minor or Major *"to any player who runs or jumps into, or charges an opponent"*, its mandatory Major and Game Misconduct *"[i]f injury results"*, and its Note *"If more than two steps or strides are taken, it shall be considered a charge"*, with 52(b)'s Major and Game Misconduct for charging a goalkeeper in the crease *"or who injures an opponent as a result of a charge"*; and 53(a)'s floor of *"[a] Minor penalty plus a Game Misconduct"*, its mandatory Major plus Game Misconduct *"[i]f a player is injured"*, its *"[a] Match penalty could also be assessed under this rule"*, and **53(b)**'s Match penalty with the Note instructing referees *"not to substitute other penalties when a player is checked from behind in any manner. This rule must be strictly enforced."* ⚠️ **A concept search of the whole book, including across line breaks, on 2 September 2026 found no windup or follow-through carve-out** — *windup*, *wind-up*, *wind up*, *shooting motion*, *backswing*, *back swing*, *follow through*, *follow-through*, *accidental*, *unintentional* and *inadvertent* were each searched; the single *"wind up"* is the glossary's slap-shot definition, and *"accidental"* appears in Rule 62 only to withhold the game misconduct, never to withhold the major. ⚠️ **Citation-resolution note, because this one is likely to be re-found and read as a contradiction:** a search of the CARHA book for *waist* hits **62(a)**, and that is **not** a waist floor on the contact rule and does not falsify the statement above that the waist floor sits in the NHL book and the IIHF 2026/27 one and nowhere else. 62(a) is a carry threshold for a different offence — checking or intimidating while carrying the stick above the opponent's waist, no contact required. ⚠️ **CARHA binds CARHA-affiliated adult leagues only** and is not a general rec-hockey standard; it is cited throughout because many of this document's readers play adult recreational hockey, and only the rules named in these notes were read. **CARHA Rule 66** (Interference/Protection of the Goalkeeper — 66(a)'s Note 2 and 66(b) in full, including its crease-position bar) and **Rules 60(c), 68 (Kick Shot) and 69 (Kicking the Puck)** were read on 14 October 2026, with the Glossary entries *Creases* and *Slew Footing* and **Rule 86(b)** · **USA Hockey Rules 403 (Major Penalties), 606 (Butt-Ending), 611 (Elbowing), 622 (Holding an Opponent, with 622(b) and (c) on the facemask) and 624 (Icing the Puck)** are cited from the 2025-29 Official Playing Rules, and **NHL and IIHF Rule 56.1 (Interference)** from the NHL 2025-2026 and IIHF 2026/27 books*

*The pre-game warm-up, fighting outside the periods of the game, the EIHL fighting and jersey rules, the EIHL bench-leaving suspension and head-butting — read 22 September 2026: [Motorpoint EIHL Casebook 26-27, Section D](https://www.eliteleague.co.uk/document/191-eihl-casebook-26-27-version-1-1-080926) — **Rule 86.6 Pre-Game Warm-Up** in full, including both lettered limbs, the paragraph exposing players, team officials, clubs and the Head Coach to suspension and/or fine, and the authorisation for referees to call penalties *"from the start of warm-up time until 30 minutes after the end of the game, if they see the offence take place"*; the **Preamble’s** shorter copy of that same authorisation, which omits the final limb; **5.6 Pre-Game warm-up on the ice** (5.6(1)’s centre-red-line clause and 5.6’s 40-to-20-minute clock); and **Rule 47 Head-Butting**, whose entire content in that book is its two printed *"[e]xamples of a double minor"* · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2025/26 (Version 1.1)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/contentimages/3_the_iihf/2025-26_iihf_rulebook_22122025-v1.pdf) and [IIHF Official Rulebook 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/rule%20book/2026-27_iihf_rule_book.pdf) — **Rule 5.6** and **Rule 47** (47.1 to 47.4), compared across the two editions and word for word identical in each; **Rule 86.6 Pre-Game Warm-Up**, whose cross-reference is to the fighting rules and not to 5.6; and **Fighting Other Than During the Periods of the Game**, which is **Rule 46.8** in the 2026/27 book and **Rule 46.9** in Version 1.1, also compared across the two — the tier is *"a major penalty for fighting and an automatic game misconduct"* in each, the 2025/26 text adding *"(5'+GMP)"*. ⚠️ **Nothing in either edition reconciles 5.6 with that rule**, and §1 states the tension rather than resolving it · [NHL Official Rules 2025-2026](https://media.d3.nhle.com/image/private/t_document/prd/slwjuaqwmuvj5bkplixo.pdf) — **Rule 47 Head-butting** (47.1 to 47.5), read 26 September 2026 · [PWHL Official Rule Book 2025-2026](https://assets.contentstack.io/v3/assets/bltebdb4296e05d53db/bltb8440fe08a5e6dc5/69178cd0a582d7db321ad107/2025-2026_PWHL_Rulebook.pdf) — **Rule 47 Head–butting** (47.1 to 47.5), read 26 September 2026 · [USA Hockey 2025-29 Official Playing Rules](https://cdn2.sportngin.com/attachments/document/945a-3442848/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules.pdf) — **Rule 502(b)** and **Rule 619** with its Note · [Hockey Canada Playing Rules 2026-2028 (PDF)](https://cdn.hockeycanada.ca/hockey-canada/Hockey-Programs/Officiating/Downloads/2026-28-hc-rulebook-e.pdf) — **Rule 2.2(j)** with its Note 1, **Rule 4.1(a)**, and **Rule 7.1** (7.1(a)(iii), 7.1(c)(i) and Interpretation 1 to Rule 7.1(a)) · [CARHA Hockey Official Rule Book 2026 (PDF)](https://carhahockey.ca/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/RuleBk-2026e.pdf) — **Rule 27(b)**, CARHA-affiliated adult leagues only · [IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27, Revision 1 (published 25/07/26)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/IHUK-In-House-Rules-2026-2027.pdf) — **searched for any warm-up conduct provision, for head-butting and for any bench-leaving or altercation provision, and it writes none of them**; the word *altercation* returns nothing there or in the IHUK junior, U10, NIHL and WNIHL *Rules of Competition*, all searched 22 September 2026, its only warm-up reference being a pre-warm-up equipment check, which is why §1 leaves NIHL, SNL, BUIHA, junior and women’s hockey under the IIHF book unmodified — Rule 5.6, the fighting rule at 46.8/46.9, and Rule 47 alike. **The EIHL fighting section additionally rests on** the Casebook’s **46.1**, **46.2 Altercation** (its *"Fighting After the Original Altercation"* game misconduct and its Department of Player Safety review), **46.2 Aggressor**, **46.10**, **46.13 Jerseys** in full, its *Fines and Suspensions* item (2), *"Leaving the Player / Penalty Bench (Also see Rule 70)"*, and item (4) on altercations *"other than during the periods of the game"*, and its *Automatic Supplementary Discipline (DOPS)* ladder with the Note attached to it. Its page footers still read *"SECTION D VERSION 1.0 010826"*, but its own version table lists *"Version 1.1 080926"*, valid from 09/26 to 07/27, as replacing Version 1.0 010826 — so the footers are left over from the earlier version and the copy read here is Version 1.1*

*Clipping and kneeing outside the four books — read 22 September 2026: [Motorpoint EIHL Casebook 26-27, Section D](https://www.eliteleague.co.uk/document/191-eihl-casebook-26-27-version-1-1-080926) — its **contents list** and its **full body**, searched by rule number and by concept: it amends twenty-two IIHF rules and **neither Rule 44 nor Rule 50 is among them**, and the strings *knee* and *clip* return nothing in it. Its **Preamble** provides that the Casebook's interpretations *"will replace or in specific cases, supersede, the ruling that is currently within the IIHF Rule Book"* and that *"[f]or all rules not mentioned in the EIHL Casebook, the official 2026 - 2027 IIHF Rulebook and IIHF Situation Handbook will serve as the official ruling"*, with updates sent *"by email, on the EIHL web site and in print"*. The copy read is Version 1.1 080926, which its own version table records as replacing Version 1.0 010826 · [IIHF Situation Handbook 2025/26 (Version 1.1, August 2025)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/officiating%20files/situation%20handbook/2025_iihf_situationhandbook_17082025-v1_1.pdf) and [IIHF Situation Handbook 2026/27](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/officiating%20files/situation%20handbook/2026-27_iihf_situation_handbook.pdf) — **Situation 44.1**, quoted above and word for word identical in the two editions, and **Rule 50**, whose whole entry in each reads *"No situation."* · [IHUK Junior Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Junior-Rules-of-Competition-2026-27.pdf), [IHUK NIHL 1 and 2 Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/NIHL-1-and-2-ROC-2026-2027-1.pdf), [IHUK WNIHL Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/WNIHL-Rules-of-Competition-2026-2027.pdf), [IHUK U10 Rules of Competition 2026-27 (PDF)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/U10-Rules-of-Competition-2026-2027.pdf) and the [IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27, Revision 1 (published 25/07/26)](https://englandicehockey.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/IHUK-In-House-Rules-2026-2027.pdf) — all five searched for both fouls and silent on each; their only *clip* is the helmet J-clip.*

*Helmets in an altercation — all four books, read 1 September 2026, and PWHL Rule 46.6 (Helmets), read 15 October 2026: [NHL Official Rules 2025-2026 (PDF)](https://media.d3.nhle.com/image/private/t_document/prd/slwjuaqwmuvj5bkplixo.pdf) — **Rule 46.6 Helmets** and **Rule 9.6**, with **51.1 and 51.2** · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2025/26 (Version 1.1)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/contentimages/3_the_iihf/2025-26_iihf_rulebook_22122025-v1.pdf) — **Rule 46.12 Helmets**, with **51.1 and 51.2** · [IIHF Official Rulebook 2026/27 (Version 1.0, June 2026)](https://blob.iihf.com/iihf-media/iihfmvc/media/downloads/rule%20book/2026-27_iihf_rule_book.pdf) — **Rule 46.6 Helmets**, the same clause renumbered, differing only in reading *"a two minute minor penalty"* where the 2025/26 text reads *"a minor penalty"*; **Rule 51 is word for word identical in the two editions**, compared 1 September 2026 · [USA Hockey 2025-29 Official Playing Rules](https://cdn2.sportngin.com/attachments/document/945a-3442848/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules.pdf) — **Rule 615(c)**, read in full: two match penalties, the game misconduct with the two-game suspension, and the game misconduct for a helmet not worn as designed. **Rule 640 Unnecessary Roughness was searched for a helmet clause and has none** · [USA Hockey Official Rules and Casebook 2025-29 (PDF)](https://cdn3.sportngin.com/attachments/document/4f1a-3442850/2025-29_USAH_Playing_Rules___Casebook.pdf) — **Rule 615, Situations 12 to 21** and the **Note applying to Situations 14-20**, read in full. **The Casebook's printed Rule 615 runs (a) to (f) exactly as the Playing Rules volume does and its situations cite the same letters**, so the one-letter offset that affects Rule 610 does not affect 615 — checked 1 September 2026. Nothing here rests on the book's Summary of Penalties, whose two 615(c) entries disagree with each other ("Match Penalty Option" against "Match Penalty (only)") · [Hockey Canada Playing Rules 2026-2028 (PDF)](https://cdn.hockeycanada.ca/hockey-canada/Hockey-Programs/Officiating/Downloads/2026-28-hc-rulebook-e.pdf) — **Rule 10.6 Illegal Equipment** (10.6(d), 10.6(f)(iii) and (iv), and **Interpretation 15 to Rule 10.6(f)(iii)(iv)**), with **Rule 4.9(a) and 4.9(b)** for what a Gross Misconduct is. **Rules 7.9 Roughing and 7.10 Fighting were read for a helmet clause and have none.** The book prints that interpretation's heading as *"Interpretation 15 Rule 10.6 (f)(iii)(iv)"*; its first ruling is quoted above in paraphrase because the copy read here prints *"came of"* rather than *"came off"*, and no second copy was available to settle whether that is the book's typo or a copying fault.*

*Boards safety — the three-case rule in §6 is this document's synthesis across two separate, unranked USA Hockey lists, its wording quoted verbatim from: [USA Hockey — Introduction To Body Contact (Coaching Education Program, PDF)](https://cdn4.sportngin.com/attachments/document/55c1-2114751/IntroductiontoBodyContact.pdf) — "Contact Confidence": A. Checked Into The Boards, B. Checked Along The Boards, C. Checked Away From The Boards (open ice — not used in the three-case rule); and the Heads Up Hockey appendix's own three-item teaching list, numbered as topics rather than ranked: "Angling In", "Hitting the Boards", "Taking a Check" · [USA Hockey — Heads Up Hockey Program Guide (PDF)](https://cdn4.sportngin.com/attachments/document/0138/6193/HUH_Program_Guide_FINAL.pdf) — "How spinal injuries happen in hockey" (cervical flexion straightening the curve; "it can occur at walking speed"), citing Charles H. Tator et al., *Spinal Injuries Due To Hockey*, Canadian Journal of Neurological Sciences 11:34–41; Session 5, "Giving A Check" (the quote sits under its "Checking from behind" subheading, which is not the session's title), for *"Even a light hit from behind could inflict a severe head or neck injury"*, carried in the same words by *Introduction To Body Contact* §5 "Checking From Behind"; and "Identifying and dealing with a spinal injury", carried in condensed and near-identical form in the *Introduction To Body Contact* Heads Up Hockey appendix — that appendix has no such heading, and its wording differs in small ways ("get help" for "your immediate role is to get help", "arms or legs" for "arms and/or legs", "arms and legs" for "arms or legs"). The quotations in §6 follow the Program Guide. **Also read in the Program Guide on 2 September 2026, for the head-up rule at the top of §6:** its **Rule One of Heads Up Hockey** — *"Keep your head up on the ice, especially when it looks like you're going to take a hit"*, which coaches are told to teach as *"the single most important thing to know and do to prevent head injuries"*, and the sentence beside it, *"most people duck when they see a hit coming, but doing so puts them in danger"* — and the **four basic safety techniques for taking a check**, of which *"Know where your opponents are"* carries the reason quoted above, that when players do, *"they'll be less likely to get checked without any warning."* ⚠️ **That superlative is a ranking of the habit, not of any game situation**, and it is USA Hockey's rather than this guide's; neither this guide nor *Introduction To Body Contact* was found to rank moments in a game by danger. The same wording and the same qualification are carried by [Playing Without the Puck](../hockey-iq/playing_without_the_puck.md). Both PDFs refetched and every string quoted here located in the documents on 31 July 2026. **Note the division of labour between the two quotes:** the walking-speed finding is USA Hockey's statement about *"the head being slightly flexed (head down) while making head-on contact with the boards or goal post"* — a head-down, head-first collision — and the guide does **not** attach it to a player turning their back. The hazard of the turned back is that it makes the contact a hit from behind, which is the sentence quoted for it. The walking-speed sentence appears in the Program Guide only; it is not in *Introduction To Body Contact*. **Note:** the same guide's Grade 1/2/3 concussion table (Colorado Medical Society, 1991) is obsolete and is deliberately not used anywhere in this guide — for concussion, follow CRT6 and the Amsterdam consensus via [Conditioning and Recovery](../off-the-ice/conditioning_and_recovery.md). · The body-play progression in §5 is from *Introduction To Body Contact*, "A Misunderstanding of Body Checking" (skating → angling → stick checks → contact confidence → body blocking → take out/rub out → solid body checks), and the one-stride open-ice constraint from its "Shoulder Check – open ice contact" entry **The goal-post material in §9 rests on the same two documents, which name the post beside the boards throughout.** The Program Guide's "How spinal injuries happen in hockey" carries it in both quoted sentences — the mechanism, *"when a player hits the boards or a goal post head on"*, and the finding, *"head-on contact with the boards or goal post"* — and its Session 3 is headed *"Good ways and bad ways to have a close encounter with a goal post, the boards, or another player."* The one-line instruction *"Hit the boards or goal posts with an arm, a leg, or anything but your head first"* is printed in *Introduction To Body Contact* under "Basic Principles of Heads Up Hockey", and §9 attributes it there. Look Up Line prints the same instruction among its own basic principles **without the Oxford comma** — *"Hit the boards or goal posts with an arm, a leg or anything but your head first"* — and that is the form §6 quotes. The Program Guide separately lists *"[a]voiding goal posts while driving to the net"*, but prints it as one of the answers it expects players to give when asked what safe hockey means rather than as an instruction of its own, so nothing here rests on it. All strings located in the documents on 1 September 2026. · [Look Up Line — Heads Up, Don't Duck](https://lookupline.org/heads-up-dont-duck/) · [Hockey Equipment Certification Council — Heads Up, Don't Duck!](http://hecc.org/headsup.html)*

*Concussion and the on-ice spinal-injury response: [Concussion Recognition Tool 6 (CRT6), Echemendia RJ et al., Br J Sports Med 2023;57:692-694 (doi:10.1136/bjsports-2023-107021)](https://bjsm.bmj.com/content/57/11/692) — the source of the three "Remember" box instructions quoted in the spinal-injury section: "Do not attempt to move the athlete (other than required for airway support) unless trained to do so", "Do not remove helmet (if present) or other equipment", and "Assume a possible spinal cord injury in all cases of head injury". ⚠️ **Do not try to search the published two-page PDF for these.** Its searchable text holds none of the tool's own words, only the journal's stamp, so a search for *helmet* comes back empty while the instruction sits plainly printed on the page. All three quotations above were located by reading the printed pages, 31 August 2026. Developed by the Concussion in Sport Group, and its own "Supported by" line names the International Olympic Committee, FEI, FIA, FIFA, the IIHF and World Rugby. The USA Hockey material cited above carries no helmet instruction of any kind — searched in full, including across line breaks, in both coaching manuals, the Playing Rules and the Casebook. [Conditioning and Recovery](../off-the-ice/conditioning_and_recovery.md) sets out CRT6's ten red flags and the hours afterwards, beside the fourteen-entry list England Ice Hockey has adopted, which [UK and England Rules](../foundation/uk_rules.md) carries in full.*

*Coaching and technique: [USA Hockey — Winning in Transition: Angling, Gaps and Creating Turnovers in Today's Game](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/988313-winning-in-transition-angling-gaps-and-creating-turnovers-in-today-s-game) (Seth Appert) · [USA Hockey — Body Contact Should Be Taught at Every Level](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/904873-body-contact-should-be-taught-at-every-level) (Guy Gosselin) · [USA Hockey — Body Contact: The (Sometimes) Forgotten Element of Skill Development](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/453192-body-contact-the-sometimes-forgotten-element-of-skill-development) (Ken Klee) · [USA Hockey — Clinic Teaches Players and Coaches How to Use Body Contact Safely and Effectively](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/873887-clinic-teaches-players-and-coaches-how-to-use-body-contact-safely-and-effectively) (Dan Jablonic; "Heads Up, Don't Duck") · [USA Hockey — Michigan Clinics Emphasize the Skills of Proper Body-Checking](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/881483-michigan-clinics-emphasize-the-skills-of-proper-body-checking) (Bob Mancini) · [USA Hockey — Net-Front Presence with Alabama-Huntsville's Mike Corbett](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/775914-net-front-presence-with-alabama-huntsville-s-mike-corbett) · [USA Hockey — Winning Battles Essential to Winning Games](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/494762-winning-battles-essential-to-winning-games) (Michele Amidon) · [USA Hockey — Puck Protection: Tips for Adults](https://www.usahockey.com/news_article/show/1168288-puck-protection-tips-for-adults) (Blaine Buterbaugh) · [How To Hockey — Tips to Safely Block a Shot](https://howtohockey.com/tips-to-safely-block-a-shot-in-hockey/) · [How To Hockey — Playing the Angles in Defense](https://howtohockey.com/playing-the-angles-in-defense/) · [How To Hockey — How to Hit Properly](https://howtohockey.com/how-to-hit-properly/) · [CoachThem — How to Defend With Angling (Danny Heath)](https://coachthem.com/blog/how-to-defend-with-angling-danny-heath) (the don't-cross-over-as-you-close point in §2)*

*Penalty-location data: [NHL public play-by-play API](https://api-web.nhle.com/v1/gamecenter/2024020001/play-by-play) — `details.descKey`, `details.xCoord`, `details.yCoord` for every penalty; the §9 figures aggregate game IDs 2024020001–2024020400 (2,967 penalties, 265 inside the net-front box — the box being 10 to 25 feet out from the goal line, i.e. 64 ≤ |x| ≤ 79, and within 10 feet either side of centre, |y| ≤ 10, in the API's own foot-based rink coordinates). Coordinates are as logged by officials and are approximate, and 400 games is not a full season.*

*Conditioning against penalty minutes — the §12 figures: [Haugen T, Hopkins W, Breitschädel F, Paulsen G, Solberg P — "Fitness Tests and Match Performance in a Male Ice Hockey National League", Int J Sports Physiol Perform, 2021;16(9):1303–1310 (PMID 33662926, doi:10.1123/ijspp.2020-0644)](https://vuir.vu.edu.au/44542/1/Fitness_tests%20_and_match_performance_in_a_male_ice_hockey_national_league.pdf). ⚠️ **The link is the free accepted manuscript in Victoria University's research repository, not the published article** — Human Kinetics puts the version of record behind a subscription. The record and the abstract were read from the Europe PMC REST API on 2 September 2026, and the abstract was confirmed the same day in the rendered text of the Human Kinetics article page the DOI resolves to. **The correlations below are Table 1 of the accepted manuscript**, read from the manuscript PDF on 2 September 2026; the version of record's full text sits behind that subscription and has not been read here. Penalty minutes per game against each test measure, all players 2008–2017, goalies excluded: age 0.25, body mass index 0.16, squat 0.14, bench press 0.23, 40-metre sprint −0.04, countermovement jump 0.05, hanging sit-ups 0.07, chin-ups 0.00, box jumps −0.09, 3000-metre run −0.01. The paper's own thresholds put anything under ±0.10 at trivial, and it gives 90% compatibility limits of about ±0.11 for the game-against-test correlations, so every endurance value above is inside the noise. ⚠️ **Three limits on how far this can be pushed.** The penalty-minute correlations appear only in Table 1 and are not discussed anywhere in the text; the study's own conclusion is about predicting match performance, not about penalties. The paper's Methods paragraph lists what was downloaded from the Norwegian Ice-Hockey Association's site as games played, goals, assists, points, plus-minus and national-team selection — **penalty minutes is named in the abstract and in Table 1 but omitted from that enumeration.** And the design is between-player and season-level, so it cannot speak to within-shift fatigue, which is the mechanism §12 describes. The search behind the negative, run against Europe PMC on 2 September 2026 with field-qualified phrases rather than the loose default parser, and with every hit read: `ABSTRACT:"penalty minutes" AND ABSTRACT:"fitness"` returns this paper alone; `TITLE_ABS:"penalties" AND TITLE_ABS:"fatigue" AND TITLE_ABS:"hockey"` returns nothing; `TITLE_ABS:"penalty minutes" AND TITLE_ABS:"ice hockey"` returns nine, of which the rest are about aggression, personality, jerseys, facial metrics or injury risk rather than conditioning. **The nearest adjacent finding, carried here only to bound the negative and not relied on anywhere in the text:** [Acquin JM, Giguère-Lemieux E, Deschamps A, Glaude-Roy J, Brunelle JF, Fait P, Corbin-Berrigan LA — "Baseline Neurotracker performance does not correlate to overall season sport performance in university varsity athletes", Front Sports Act Living, 2025;7:1608463 (PMID 41234217, doi:10.3389/fspor.2025.1608463)](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC12605341) found that in its 34 male hockey players, a pre-season multiple-object-tracking score correlated negatively with **total penalty minutes at r = −0.375 and games played at r = −0.381**, both p < 0.05, and with nothing else it measured; its conclusion is that the tool has no use as a performance predictor. ⚠️ **Read that one against its own tables, because its abstract does not match them — and it does not match them in two places, not one.** The abstract reports a significant *penalty minutes per game* correlation of −0.39; the paper's Table 3 gives that figure as **−0.28 at p = 0.11, which is not significant** — the two significant columns are games played and total penalty minutes, and games played is the one the abstract omits. **The abstract also disagrees about *total* penalty minutes**, where it gives −0.40, the Results text gives −0.375 and Table 3 gives −0.38. The last two are one value at two precisions; the abstract's is neither of them. **The figures quoted above are the Results text's, not the abstract's.** ⚠️ **And the mechanism the paper offers for the penalty finding is not its own**: it credits it to Poltavski and Biberdorf, its reference 16, which has not been read here. This is a rested perceptual-cognitive baseline in 34 players, not conditioning and not fatigue, so it is carried here only to bound the negative above and nothing in this guide rests on it.*

*Leverage and stance (nearest quantification, non-hockey): [Green A, Coopoo Y, Tee J, McKinon W — "A review of the biomechanical determinants of rugby scrummaging performance", S Afr J Sports Med, 2019;31(1) (PMID 36817990)](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36817990/) — **open-access full text read via Europe PMC (PMC9924573) on 28 July 2026**; note that the hip/knee/ankle correlations quoted in §7 are the review's reference [13], **not the review's own data** · [Wu WL, Chang JJ, Wu JH, et al. — "An investigation of rugby scrummaging posture and individual maximum pushing force", J Strength Cond Res, 2007;21(1):251–258](https://doi.org/10.1519/00124278-200702000-00045) — **the actual source of r=−0.47 / −0.51 / −0.70**; its abstract, read via Europe PMC (PMID 17313278) on 10 September 2026, scopes those correlations to parallel feet, reports the opposite sign for rear-leg hip angle in cross-feet postures, and reports maximum pushing force *"significantly lower in posture I (36% body height) than in posture II (38%) and posture III (40%)"* across ten national players · [Green A, Kerr S, Dafkin C, Olivier B, McKinon W — "A lower body height and wider foot stance are positively associated with the generation of individual scrummaging forces in rugby", Int J Perform Anal Sport, 2017;17(1–2):177–189, doi:10.1080/24748668.2017.1309094](https://doi.org/10.1080/24748668.2017.1309094)*

*Fall biomechanics (cited only to bound the locked-arm claim, not as hockey evidence): [Chou PH, Chou YL, Lin CJ, et al. — "Effect of elbow flexion on upper extremity impact forces during a fall", Clinical Biomechanics, 2001;16(10):888–894 (PMID 11733126)](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11733126/) · [Morrissette C, Park PJ, Lehman RA, Popkin CA — "Cervical Spine Injuries in the Ice Hockey Player", Global Spine Journal, 2021;11(8):1299–1306 (PMID 33203240)](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33203240/)*

*Unverified or single-source, flagged inline: the warning that bracing a locked, straight arm against the boards breaks wrists and collarbones appears in no USA Hockey, HECC or Look Up Line document. **What was searched, rather than that it held:** *Introduction To Body Contact* and the *Heads Up Hockey Program Guide* searched in full, and both organisations' *Heads Up, Don't Duck* pages refetched 9 September 2026 (HTTP 200 each; the older `hecc.org/headsup.html` address redirects to `hecc.org/safety-guidelines/heads-up-dont-duck/`). ⚠️ **And what those four do carry runs the other way: all three bodies name the arm as a thing to meet the boards with.** USA Hockey's own drill has players *"make contact with the boards and glass with their extended arms, forearms, side of body and legs… but with no head contact at all"*, with the arms out forming a *"three-part 'shock absorber'"*, and its basic principles carry *"[h]it the boards or goal posts with an arm, a leg, or anything but your head first"* — Look Up Line prints the same instruction among its own basic principles, without the Oxford comma. ⚠️ **Neither body numbers it**: in both, "Rule One" is the ducking prohibition, and the arm-and-leg line is a separate unnumbered principle. **The two print Rule One differently, so quote whichever book you are citing** — *Introduction To Body Contact* has *"Rule One: Heads Up – Don't Duck!"* with an en dash, while Look Up Line's own principles list has *"Rule One: Head up – Don't duck!"* and the prose above that list writes the rule as *"Heads Up — Don't Duck!"*, which is the *Heads Up Hockey Program Guide*'s sentence word for word. HECC's page asks a player to *"[i]f possible, use your hands, arms, shoulders or facemask to cushion the impact."* **Those are answers to a different question**, which is why §6 keeps both: they say which part of you meets the wall instead of your head, where the disputed warning is about whether the elbow is locked when it gets there. The manual's own word for the arms is a *shock absorber*, which is a thing that gives; reading the two as compatible on that basis is §6's reconciliation and is labelled there as one. The warning itself is retained in §6 as labelled coaching craft. The nearest evidence is fall biomechanics rather than hockey: Chou and colleagues measured elbow mediolateral shear 68% higher in extension than flexion during forward falls onto the hand, but found no axial-force difference, and no study has applied that to board collisions. **On being hit from behind**, USA Hockey does publish a receiving-player body position for being checked while facing the boards, and its *Heads Up Hockey* guide tells a player who "can't skate through a check" to keep "arms up, knees bent and, of course, their heads up." No published treatment gives a technique premised on genuinely zero warning; beyond that the hazard is addressed through the checker's obligations and the victim's awareness. **The strong form of the cross-checking claim is not supported by the call data:** over 400 games of the 2024-25 season, cross-checking is the smallest of the seven infraction types broken out at the net front rather than the most-called, and at **1.08×** it is called there at essentially its league-wide rate rather than being concentrated there at all; the penalties that genuinely concentrate in that box are hooking (2.10×) and slashing (1.76×). Those figures and the full method are set out in §9. USA Hockey's Standard of Play naming the net-front cross-check as its leading example remains an enforcement emphasis, which is how it is presented. The "over 70 per cent of goals scored within two stick-lengths of the net" figure is a single coach's account of one season at one NCAA programme, not a league-wide statistic — and the same play-by-play feed contradicts it at NHL level: over 400 games of 2024-25, and of the 2,498 goals in them that carry coordinates, the true share within two stick-lengths is **26.8%**, with 70% reached only at about thirty feet. It is retained in §9 as a quotation with the contradiction stated beside it, rather than deleted, and is not offered here as a statistic. The USA Hockey interview was refetched on 28 August 2026 and the quoted sentence located in the rendered page; **the two claims §9 used to open with are not Corbett's.** He says nothing about penalties anywhere in that interview, and he describes his own team's season rather than where goals are scored in general. **"Most penalties are taken" at the net front was also contradicted by this document's own call data** — 265 of 2,967 penalties fall inside the net-front box — so it is not stated any more; the box's real signature is hooking and slashing, which is set out in the table. The scale point is separate: the crease front is a sub-area of the slot, and the claim that the **slot** takes most goals belongs to [Shooting](shooting.md), with the Sportlogiq limits that document carries. Hockey Canada's checking-skills coaching resources (as distinct from its rulebook) are not publicly available, so all Hockey Canada claims here are sourced to the Playing Rules — and **Rule 7.4 does carry the two-stride charging test** in both the 2024-2026 and 2026-2028 editions. NHL rule numbering and wording here are taken from the NHL Official Rules 2025-2026. **The British body-checking age is not in either rule book, and is taken here from the competition regulations instead.** The IIHF Rule Book 2025/26 was searched in full for any age or category restriction on body checking and contains none outside women's Rule 101.1; its junior section (Rules 200–202) covers age eligibility, facial protection and one penalty rule and says nothing about contact. The In-House Rules 2025-26 Rev 1 were read in full and contain no reference to body checking, body contact or non-contact play in any section. This guide therefore does **not** take a British threshold from either rule book and does **not** assume the USA Hockey 12U or Hockey Canada U13 line transfers; the answer lives in individual competition regulations, and the four IHUK Rules of Competition 2026-27 cited below — the junior, U10, NIHL 1 and 2 and WNIHL editions — are quoted in §1, and nothing beyond them is taken from any competition regulation. Rule Bulletins have not been obtained, and nor have the regulations of the EIHL, BUIHA, NIHL National, the PNIHL, the SNL or Scottish domestic competitions outside the IHUK junior structure. ⚠️ **That gap is not confined to competitions IHUK does not run**: the NIHL 1 and 2 document states that *"NIHL leagues are solely owned and operated by IHUK"*, which covers NIHL National, whose own regulations were never obtained. The **EIHL Casebook 26-27** has now been read, on 22 September 2026, and what it changed is set out inline: its boarding, charging and elbowing sections, its Rule 46 fighting sections (46.1, 46.2 altercation, 46.2 aggressor, 46.10 instigator, 46.13 jerseys), its Rule 47 head-butting examples, its Rule 63, its Rule 64 diving section, its Rule 69 addition, and its pre-game warm-up rules at **86.6** and **5.6**. ⚠️ **Two things about the warm-up rules are the Casebook’s own and are stated inline rather than smoothed over:** its authorisation for referees to call penalties from the start of warm-up appears twice, and the Preamble copy omits the *"if they see the offence take place"* limb that the copy at 86.6 carries, so 86.6 is the version quoted here; and 86.6’s second lettered limb says warm-up violations *"cannot be enforced by on-ice officials because they are not on the ice"* while its first limb puts the contact and red-line rules with *"the standby referee who oversees the warmup"*, which is how the two are read together in §1. **The IIHF’s own Rule 5.6 was read in both editions for the comparison** and is identical in each; **the IHUK In-House Rules 2026-27 were searched for any warm-up conduct provision and contain none** beyond a pre-warm-up equipment check, which is why nothing in §1 carries the Casebook’s warm-up rules beyond the EIHL. **Its Preamble is the reason any of it matters**: the Casebook's interpretations *“will replace or in specific cases, supersede, the ruling that is currently within the IIHF Rule Book”*, and the IIHF book governs only *“[f]or all rules not mentioned in the EIHL Casebook”*. ⚠️ **Three things it does not do, stated because the gaps above are unchanged by it.** It sets **no body-checking format and no age** — a search of the whole document, including across line breaks, for *check*, *checking* and *bodycheck* returns **zero**, and it is a playing-rule document rather than a *Rules of Competition*, so the EIHL checking gap named above stands. It writes **nothing on checking from behind (Rule 43) or illegal checks to the head or neck (Rule 48)**, so for an EIHL reader those rules are the IIHF's unmodified. And its **Section 11, Women’s Ice Hockey, reads in full *“Not applicable to EIHL Competition”***. **No British competition regulation held here adopts the Casebook** — the word *Casebook* returns zero in the IHUK In-House Rules for 2026-27 and 2025-26, in the England Ice Hockey Rules and Regulations, and in all four IHUK *Rules of Competition* — so none of it is carried into anything this document says about the NIHL, the SNL, BUIHA or junior hockey. **On the net-front lift in §9, no penalty is asserted and the caution is coaching caution.** A whole-book search, including across line breaks, of the USA Hockey Playing Rules, the USA Hockey Casebook and the Hockey Canada Playing Rules on 1 September 2026 returns no clause about extending the body upward or outward: the only *"outward"* in either book is the knee, under kneeing (USA Hockey Rule 628, Hockey Canada Rule 7.8), and Hockey Canada's two instances of *"upwards"* are a slash and a cross-check deflecting into the head. **The NHL is the fourth book and it does write one**, at **Rule 48.1(i)**, read 1 September 2026 — a hit is judged on, among other things, *"[w]hether the player attempted to hit squarely through the opponent's body and the head was not 'picked' as a result of poor timing, poor angle of approach, or unnecessary extension of the body upward or outward"* — which is the clause §5 quotes. **IIHF Rule 48.1(III)** is its counterpart, word for word identical in the 2025/26 (Version 1.1) and 2026/27 editions, compared 1 September 2026. ⚠️ **Neither reaches a box-out**: both sit inside the illegal check to the head or neck, the NHL's opening on *"[a] hit resulting in contact with an opponent's head"* and the IIHF's chapeau confining its list to *"when a Player checks an opponent"*. So the negative that matters is the narrow one — **no book here writes a penalty for the lift on its own** — and not a claim that only one book names the extension. **Keeping a moved body off the path across the goalmouth is likewise coaching caution drawn from the sourced hazard, not a rule any book writes** — walking a screener out at all can be called interference, which the books do write. What the books do write is the goal frame beside the boards in checking from behind (USA Hockey 608(b), Hockey Canada 7.5(c), IIHF 43.1) and in charging (NHL 42.1, IIHF 42.1, Hockey Canada 7.4, in identical words; USA Hockey's Rule 607 Note names neither), all read 1 September 2026 — with **NHL 43.2** and **IIHF 43.2** both read the same day for the no-minor sentence, the NHL's *"[t]here is no provision for a minor penalty for checking from behind"* and the IIHF's *"[t]his infraction is considered serious and dangerous, therefore there is no option to award a minor penalty for 'Checking from Behind'"* (the book hyphenates *"Be-hind"* across a line). **No injury rate for net-front or goal-post collisions is given anywhere in this document, because no study giving one was found in this corpus or its sources.***

*The IIHF’s absent match penalty, and what its ceiling actually costs — read in the rulebooks on 25 September 2026. **IIHF Official Rule Book 2025/26 (v1.1)**: **Rule 20.4**, where a major listed in the Reference Tables carries an *“Automatic Game Misconduct”* and the Player *“shall be ruled off the ice for the balance of the game, but a substitute shall be permitted to replace the suspended Player after five (5) minutes have elapsed”*; **Rule 23.1**, where a game misconduct penalty *“involves the suspension of a Player for the balance of the game, but a substitute is permitted to replace the Player removed”*; **Rule 43.3**, whose discretion attaches to the major and not to the game misconduct — *“The Referee, at their discretion, shall assess a major penalty and an automatic game misconduct penalty”*; **Rule 43.4**, *“[i]f deemed appropriate, Supplementary Discipline can be applied by the Proper Authorities at their discretion”*, routing to **Rule 28**; and **Appendix IV Table 6**, headed *“Summary of major penalties that result in an automatic game misconduct”*, which lists Checking from Behind at 43.3 — the **2026/27** edition merging it into **Table 5**, where that row ticks **MAJOR + GMP** and leaves **MAJOR ONLY** blank. **That the IIHF writes no match penalty was re-tested rather than assumed:** whole-book searches of both editions, including across line breaks, for “match penalt”, “gross misconduct”, “expelled” and “expulsion” each return zero. Against it, [NHL Official Rules 2025-2026 (PDF)](https://media.d3.nhle.com/image/private/t_document/prd/slwjuaqwmuvj5bkplixo.pdf) **Rule 21.1** — *“A match penalty involves the suspension of a player for the balance of the game and the offender shall be ordered to the dressing room immediately”* — with **21.2** permitting a substitute *“after five (5) minutes playing time has elapsed”*, so the two books reach the same on-ice outcome by different names. **What sits above that on-ice ceiling was read on 25 September 2026 and is why this page no longer calls the major a cap.** Each of the other acts discussed here carries its own *Disciplinary Measures* clause routing to **Rule 28**, in wording identical but for 52.3's missing full stops — *“If deemed appropriate, Supplementary Discipline can be applied by the Proper Authorities at their discretion”* — at **41.5** boarding, **42.5** charging, **45.5** elbowing, **48.4** head contact, **49.4** kicking, **52.3** slew-footing, **58.4** butt-ending, **59.6** cross-checking, **61.6** slashing and **62.4** spearing. **Rule 20.7** requires the Referee to report *“all major penalties and the surrounding circumstances”* to the Proper Authorities, **23.2** sends a game misconduct to them *“which shall have full power to impose further disciplinary measures”*, and **28.1** lets them assess additional discipline *“whether or not such offense has been penalized by the Referee(s)”*. **23.5** names a *Disciplinary Measures – Stick Infractions Category*, alongside 23.3 General, 23.4 Abuse of Officials and 23.6 Physical Infractions, and refers each to the IIHF Disciplinary Code. ⚠️ **The IIHF's current Disciplinary Regulations (in effect 6 October 2025) write no per-offence tariff, and their §10 suspensions govern IIHF Events only, so no suspension length or tariff is stated anywhere on this page** — what was verified is that the route exists and that the referral is mandatory, not what it costs.*
